Um... well, I guess I just feel, you know, a little overly full? Like anyone would if they had a big meal, really. But... I mean, if I'm being completely honest, and I don't want to sound dramatic or anything... a lot of nights I just keep eating until my stomach gets really tight.

Um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both? I mean, nobody likes eating a bunch of junk food, right? But... yeah, if I'm being really honest, and I hope this doesn't sound too weird... it's mostly the control thing.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... exercise excessively? No, no, I don't do that. I mean, I'm on my feet a lot at the store, like I said, ringing people up and bagging groceries, so I get some movement in that way. But I don't go to the gym for hours or go running or anything like that to try and work off what I ate. 

I guess I just... I don't really have the energy for it after it happens, to be honest. I mostly just feel really tired and gross, so I usually just go to bed or sit on the couch. 

Is that okay? I mean, I know exercise is good for you, and maybe I *should* be doing more of it, but I promise I'm not doing it in a crazy way to make up for the food. I'm trying to be really good about not doing anything extreme like that. Does that sound like a good thing? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers.

Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I mean, it's not every single day, I promise. I try really hard to just eat a normal dinner most nights. But... I guess for the last several months, it's been happening maybe... three or four nights most weeks? I know that sounds like a lot, and I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart or anything. \n\nIt's just... well, things have been a little tough, and I started getting these foreclosure calls a while back. I'm getting it all sorted out, I promise, but... honestly, I can't remember a single week since those calls started where I didn't end up doing this at least once. I know I should handle my problems better, but it's just a stress thing, I'm sure. A lot of people eat a little extra when they're stressed out, don't they? I hope that's an okay answer.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I just feel, you know, a little overly full? I know I shouldn't let it get to me so much, and I'm really trying to be better about it. Um... my self-worth? Like, how I feel about myself? I guess... I mean, I try not to let it get to me too much. 

I know I shouldn't base my whole worth on whether I ate too many chips or whatever. I try to be a good person, you know? I work hard at the store, I try to be nice to people... I want to focus on those things. 

But... yeah, I guess when I have one of those bad days where I can't stop eating, I do feel pretty down on myself. It's hard not to feel a little disappointed when you mess up. But I don't think it's, like, my *whole* identity or anything. I really try to stay positive. 

Is that normal? To feel a little bad about yourself when you lose control, but still know you're a good person underneath it all? I hope that's a healthy way to look at it. I'm really trying to have a good attitude about everything.

Just like anyone would if they slipped up on a diet, I think. I mean, it's not like I hate myself or anything like that. I just... \n\nWell, honestly, I feel really guilty. I look at all the empty wrappers and the bags and I just feel so ashamed that I couldn't stop. I feel kind of pathetic, honestly, like I have no willpower at all. But I'm sure everybody feels a little guilty when they eat junk food, right? That's a pretty normal reaction, isn't it? I just... I know I shouldn't let it get to me so much, and I'm really trying to be better about it. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yeah, I guess so. I mean, usually it's when I'm stressed about the house or the bills, like I mentioned. But... honestly, sometimes I'm not even really upset about anything specific? \n\nLike, sometimes I'll just be sitting there, and I feel totally fine, but I still end up doing it anyway. It's not always because I'm having a bad day or anything. I don't know if that's normal or not. Is it okay that it happens even when I'm not feeling particularly stressed out? I hope I'm explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Is the guilt you feel after these episodes more about losing control while eating, rather than just eating unhealthy foods?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both? I mean, nobody likes eating a bunch of junk food, right? But... yeah, if I'm being really honest, and I hope this doesn't sound too weird... it's mostly the control thing. \n\nIt's like... once I take that very first bite, it just feels like something inside me clicks off. And even while I'm sitting there, literally telling myself, 'Demetrius, stop, you really don't need any more of this,' my hands just keep reaching for the next thing. It's like I can't stop myself, even when I want to. I know that probably sounds like a terrible excuse, and I really am trying to get a handle on it. I hope you don't think I'm just making excuses for bad habits. Does that make sense, what I'm trying to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a sense of pleasure or enjoyment while you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, pleasure? I guess... I mean, food tastes good, right? So there's a little bit of that at first, maybe? Like, anyone enjoys a good snack, I think. \n\nBut... honestly, no, not really. It's not like I'm sitting there having a great time or anything. It's more just... I don't know, a blur? Like I'm just trying to get it down as fast as I can. I'm not really savoring it or enjoying the flavor, if that makes sense. I hope that's the right answer... I mean, I know I shouldn't be eating like that anyway, but it's definitely not because I'm just having a fun time eating. Does that sound like what usually happens?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any sense of relief after these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... relief? I mean... maybe a little bit? Like, sometimes when I'm really stressed about work or something, just taking a minute to eat kind of... distracts me, I guess? \n\nBut... no, not really. Honestly, it's mostly just feeling really bad afterward. Like, I don't feel better at all, I just feel guilty and... well, gross, to be honest. I hope I'm not disappointing you by saying that. I know some people eat to feel better, and I guess I wish it worked that way for me, but it really doesn't. Is it okay that I don't feel relieved? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, control? I don't want you to think I just completely give up or anything. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest—and I think I mentioned this a little bit earlier—it's like... once I take that very first bite, it just feels like something inside me clicks off. And even while I'm sitting there, literally telling myself, 'Demetrius, stop, you really don't need any more of this,' my hands just keep reaching for the next thing. It's like I can't stop myself, even when I want to. I know I said that before, and I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just repeating myself to make excuses. I really am trying my best to manage it. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to diet or restrict what you eat on days after these episodes as a way to make up for overeating?\n\nPatient: Um... diet? Well, I mean, I try to eat healthy, you know? Like, I'll try to have a salad or maybe just skip breakfast the next day, just to balance things out. I think a lot of people do that, right? Just trying to be responsible after a big meal? \n\nBut... as far as really strict dieting or fasting or anything like that to make up for it... no, not really. I don't really do anything extreme. I hope that's okay to say. I know some people go on these crazy diets, but I just... I don't really do that. I just try to get back to normal, I guess. Is that what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit after one of these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... vomit? Oh, no. No, I've never done that. I know some people do that when they eat too much, and I guess I understand why they might feel like they need to, but... no, I couldn't do that. \n\nI mean, I just kind of sit with how awful I feel afterward, like I was saying before. I don't try to... undo it like that. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to be completely honest with you, and I promise I'm not doing anything like that. Is it helpful that I don't do that? I just want to make sure I'm doing everything I can to help you understand what's going on with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... weight changes? Is that okay to talk about?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you start, or do you feel like you're unable to stop even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's a little hard to stop sometimes. I mean, I try to. I really do. But once I take that first bite, it's like... I don't know, it feels like something in me just clicks off? And even while I'm sitting there telling myself, 'Demetrius, stop, you don't need any more,' my hands just keep reaching for the next thing in the bag. It's probably just because I'm so hungry after work, right? That's normal, isn't it? I just... I don't want you to think I'm totally out of control or anything. I just get a little carried away sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I mean, it's not every single day, I promise. I try really hard to just eat a normal dinner most nights. But... I guess for the last several months, it's been happening maybe... three or four nights most weeks? I know that sounds like a lot, and I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart or anything. \n\nIt's just... well, things have been a little tough, and I started getting these foreclosure calls a while back. I'm getting it all sorted out, I promise, but... honestly, I can't remember a single week since those calls started where I didn't end up doing this at least once. I know I should handle my problems better, but it's just a stress thing, I'm sure. A lot of people eat a little extra when they're stressed out, don't they? I hope that's an okay answer.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel after one of these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I just feel, you know, a little overly full? Like anyone would if they had a big meal, really. But... I mean, if I'm being completely honest, and I don't want to sound dramatic or anything... a lot of nights I just keep eating until my stomach gets really tight. \n\nIt actually gets to where it's hurting so bad that I have to just lean the seat back in my car, or if I make it inside, I just have to lie perfectly still in bed and wait for it to pass. It's probably just normal indigestion from eating too fast, right? I'm sure I just overdo it a bit. I hope it's okay that I shared that, I just don't want you to think I'm making a huge deal out of a stomach ache.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel about yourself after one of these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I just feel... you know, a little disappointed in myself? Just like anyone would if they slipped up on a diet, I think. I mean, it's not like I hate myself or anything like that. I just... \n\nWell, honestly, I feel really guilty. I look at all the empty wrappers and the bags and I just feel so ashamed that I couldn't stop. I feel kind of pathetic, honestly, like I have no willpower at all. But I'm sure everybody feels a little guilty when they eat junk food, right? That's a pretty normal reaction, isn't it? I just... I know I shouldn't let it get to me so much, and I'm really trying to be better about it. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yeah, I guess so. I mean, usually it's when I'm stressed about the house or the bills, like I mentioned. But... honestly, sometimes I'm not even really upset about anything specific? \n\nLike, sometimes I'll just be sitting there, and I feel totally fine, but I still end up doing it anyway. It's not always because I'm having a bad day or anything. I don't know if that's normal or not. Is it okay that it happens even when I'm not feeling particularly stressed out? I hope I'm explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Is the guilt you feel after these episodes more about losing control while eating, rather than just eating unhealthy foods?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess it's a little bit of both? I mean, nobody likes eating a bunch of junk food, right? But... yeah, if I'm being really honest, and I hope this doesn't sound too weird... it's mostly the control thing. \n\nIt's like... once I take that very first bite, it just feels like something inside me clicks off. And even while I'm sitting there, literally telling myself, 'Demetrius, stop, you really don't need any more of this,' my hands just keep reaching for the next thing. It's like I can't stop myself, even when I want to. I know that probably sounds like a terrible excuse, and I really am trying to get a handle on it. I hope you don't think I'm just making excuses for bad habits. Does that make sense, what I'm trying to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a sense of pleasure or enjoyment while you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, pleasure? I guess... I mean, food tastes good, right? So there's a little bit of that at first, maybe? Like, anyone enjoys a good snack, I think. \n\nBut... honestly, no, not really. It's not like I'm sitting there having a great time or anything. It's more just... I don't know, a blur? Like I'm just trying to get it down as fast as I can. I'm not really savoring it or enjoying the flavor, if that makes sense. I hope that's the right answer... I mean, I know I shouldn't be eating like that anyway, but it's definitely not because I'm just having a fun time eating. Does that sound like what usually happens?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any sense of relief after these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... relief? I mean... maybe a little bit? Like, sometimes when I'm really stressed about work or something, just taking a minute to eat kind of... distracts me, I guess? \n\nBut... no, not really. Honestly, it's mostly just feeling really bad afterward. Like, I don't feel better at all, I just feel guilty and... well, gross, to be honest. I hope I'm not disappointing you by saying that. I know some people eat to feel better, and I guess I wish it worked that way for me, but it really doesn't. Is it okay that I don't feel relieved? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, control? I mean, I try to be, you know? I really do try to keep things reasonable, like anyone else would. I don't want you to think I just completely give up or anything. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest—and I think I mentioned this a little bit earlier—it's like... once I take that very first bite, it just feels like something inside me clicks off. And even while I'm sitting there, literally telling myself, 'Demetrius, stop, you really don't need any more of this,' my hands just keep reaching for the next thing. It's like I can't stop myself, even when I want to. I know I said that before, and I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just repeating myself to make excuses. I really am trying my best to manage it. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever tried to diet or restrict what you eat on days after these episodes as a way to make up for overeating?\n\nPatient: Um... diet? Well, I mean, I try to eat healthy, you know? Like, I'll try to have a salad or maybe just skip breakfast the next day, just to balance things out. I think a lot of people do that, right? Just trying to be responsible after a big meal? \n\nBut... as far as really strict dieting or fasting or anything like that to make up for it... no, not really. I don't really do anything extreme. I hope that's okay to say. I know some people go on these crazy diets, but I just... I don't really do that. I just try to get back to normal, I guess. Is that what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit after one of these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... vomit? Oh, no. No, I've never done that. I know some people do that when they eat too much, and I guess I understand why they might feel like they need to, but... no, I couldn't do that. \n\nI mean, I just kind of sit with how awful I feel afterward, like I was saying before. I don't try to... undo it like that. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to be completely honest with you, and I promise I'm not doing anything like that. Is it helpful that I don't do that? I just want to make sure I'm doing everything I can to help you understand what's going on with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... weight changes? Just going up and down a few pounds depending on the week or whatever. I haven't really noticed any big, significant changes that I'd say are directly because of... well, because of the eating. \n\nI try to stay somewhat active when I can, being on my feet a lot at work and all. It's like I can't stop myself, even when I really want to. \n\nI feel so bad admitting that, because it sounds like I just don't have any willpower or something. I try so hard to be good, I really do. Is it normal to feel like that? Like you're just watching yourself do it but you can't hit the brakes? I hope it doesn't make me sound completely crazy. I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever exercised excessively to try to burn off calories after one of these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... exercise excessively? No, no, I don't do that. I mean, I'm on my feet a lot at the store, like I said, ringing people up and bagging groceries, so I get some movement in that way. But I don't go to the gym for hours or go running or anything like that to try and work off what I ate. \n\nI guess I just... I don't really have the energy for it after it happens, to be honest. I mostly just feel really tired and gross, so I usually just go to bed or sit on the couch. \n\nIs that okay? I mean, I know exercise is good for you, and maybe I *should* be doing more of it, but I promise I'm not doing it in a crazy way to make up for the food. I'm trying to be really good about not doing anything extreme like that. Does that sound like a good thing? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever fasted or severely restricted your food intake for a period of time after one of these episodes of eating a lot of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Um... fasted? Like, not eating at all? Oh, no, I don't do anything like that. I mean, I know some people do those juice cleanses or starve themselves after they mess up, but I really try not to do that. \n\nI might, you know, try to eat a little lighter the next day—maybe just have a salad for lunch or skip a snack—just to try and be good and get back on track. But I don't stop eating completely or do anything severe. I know that's not healthy, and I really want to do things the right way. \n\nI just try to eat normally again as soon as I can. Is that the right way to handle it? I hope so. I just want to make sure I'm taking care of myself the way I'm supposed to, you know? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your self-worth is tied to your ability to control what you eat?\n\nPatient: Um... my self-worth? Like, how I feel about myself? I guess... I mean, I try not to let it get to me too much. \n\nI know I shouldn't base my whole worth on whether I ate too many chips or whatever. I try to be a good person, you know? I work hard at the store, I try to be nice to people... I want to focus on those things. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess when I have one of those bad days where I can't stop eating, I do feel pretty down on myself. I just try to wake up the next day and start over. \n\nThat's the right way to think about it, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm not doing anything that would make things worse.
